_source line 268 · 2026-05-16T05:25:37.980Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Interesting findings on westd-21461:

**GPU 0**: 90% util, 15.3GB — 1 process (PID 5576, 15.3GB). This is the Mistral SciERC constrained eval. 15.3GB for Mistral-7B in bf16 is correct (~14GB model + KV cache).

**GPU 1**: 94% util, 30.6GB — **2 processes** (PID 6284: 15.3GB + PID 6794: 15.3GB). This means two Mistral models are loaded on GPU 1!


The registry shows wrapper PIDs (6126 for SciERC constrained on cuda:0, 6279 for DocRED unconstrained on cuda:1), but nvidia-smi is reporting the actual child process PIDs instead. PID 5576 on GPU 0 corresponds to the SciERC constrained job, while PIDs 6284 and 6794 on GPU 1 are likely the child processes spawned by the DocRED wrapper.

The real concern is why there are two processes on GPU 1 each consuming 15.3GB — that's double the expected memory for a single Mistral-7B model. PID 6284 is close enough to the wrapper PID 6279 that it's probably the main process, but PID 6794 is unexplained. It could be a subprocess spawned during execution, a duplicate model load, or a stale process that didn't clean up properly.

I should verify whether this is expected behavior from the grammar-constrained generation setup or if it's actually a bug causing unnecessary memory duplication. I also need to check if PID 5576 belongs to the SciERC constrained experiment and whether there's a stale process lingering on GPU 1 from a previous run. to any known experiment. The docred_unconstrained wrapper is PID 6279, so 6284 is likely its child process on the GPU. PID 6794 is much higher and could be a grammar compilation or another subprocess, while 5576 might be leftover from an earlier finalization worker. The 30.6GB across both processes isn't critical on a 96GB card, but the pattern does suggest the model might be loaded twice, which could impact inference speed. I'll note this as something to investigate but it's not urgent given the available headroom.

The experiments are running well with solid GPU utilization across both servers. The worker successfully set up westd-21461, submitted both Mistral experiments, and achieved full saturation on all four GPUs—that's the key win here. I'll wrap this up with a brief summary.
```

```text
2. **w6_mistral_docred_unconstrained KILLED** with EXIT_CODE=129 (SIGHUP): This one was terminated externally, not a natural crash.
3. Both experiments have exit files indicating they've already finished, but the GPU processes still show active memory usage, which suggests there might be orphaned processes lingering from the crashes that haven't been cleaned up properly.

The registry is out of sync with reality—both experiments have actually failed, not running as it claims. Looking at the exit codes: scierc_constrained failed with EXIT_CODE=1 due to an xgrammar type error, while docred_unconstrained was killed with SIGHUP. The xgrammar issue points to a library compatibility problem on westd-21461 that doesn't exist on westd-16980 where the earlier constrained experiments succeeded. Since both experiments on this machine failed and the xgrammar incompatibility will block further progress, I need to address this dependency mismatch.
```

```text
1. **w6_mistral_scierc_constrained**: EXIT_CODE=1 at 05:23:47Z — crashed almost immediately after launch (submitted at 05:23:37Z, crashed 10 seconds later). xgrammar type mismatch error.

2. **w6_mistral_docred_unconstrained**: EXIT_CODE=129 (SIGHUP) at 05:25:35Z — killed ~2 minutes after submission (submitted at 05:23:40Z).

3. **Orphaned GPU processes**: Still 2 processes using GPU memory:
   - PID 5576 on GPU 0 (15.3GB) — likely from a previous test run or the worker's model loading test, not from the registered experiments.
   - PID 6794 on GPU 1 (15.3GB) — probably orphaned from the killed docred_unconstrained job.

These orphaned processes are consuming 30.5GB of GPU memory total and need to be terminated.

The registry shows both experiments as RUNNING even though they've already exited—the on_failed hooks should trigger but may not have been detected yet. Looking at the actual failures, westd-21461 hit an xgrammar type mismatch error that westd-16980 didn't encounter, pointing to an environment version difference. The unconstrained experiment was killed with SIGHUP (EXIT_CODE=129), possibly by the worker after detecting the constrained job's crash. Both westd-21461 experiments failed due to this environment mismatch, while westd-16980's constrained runs succeeded.

The key issues are the orphaned GPU processes still running, the xgrammar incompatibility blocking constrained experiments on westd-21461, and the stale registry that hasn't updated from RUNNING status. I need to escalate this immediately.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The logs still only show pad_token_id warnings — no progress lines `[N/998]` yet for either DocRED experiment. This is expected since DocRED has 998 samples with 96 relation types, and the grammar compilation phase takes significant time (~9 min for the constrained mode based on previous Llama experience). The unconstrained mode shouldn't need grammar compilation though.

Let me check the actual progress by looking at the full log head to see if there are progress lines buried above the pad_token_id warnings.

Actually, looking more carefully: I used `grep -E "^\["` which would match lines starting with `[`. 

The output shows `[transformers]` prefix lines but no `[N/998]` progress lines. The `[transformers]` lines match the pattern, so there really are no progress lines yet. The unconstrained experiment has been running for about 8 minutes since ~05:18 UTC, and with DocRED's longer documents likely taking 10-20s per sample, I should be seeing progress by now unless model loading is still happening. But the pad_token_id warnings appearing per-generation suggest the model is actively generating, so something's off with the progress reporting.

The progress lines should start with `[` based on the SciERC pattern, so my grep would have caught them if they existed. With ~30 pad_token_id warnings already logged, roughly 30 samples might have been processed. Since these experiments were submitted via nohup rather than the standard tmux wrapper, the output redirection might be different—the progress lines could be going to a different stream or the log format on this server might work differently than expected.

Both GPU processes are running with models loaded and generating (evidenced by the pad_token_id warnings), so they appear functional. The real problems are: the w6_mistral_scierc_constrained registry is stale—it shows RUNNING but the process actually crashed with an xgrammar type error, so the registry needs updating to FAILED and the experiment needs to be re-run after the xgrammar patch is applied. The missing progress lines are a monitoring inconvenience but not critical, and there's likely an issue with how nohup is handling the backend output.

The nohup backend also bypasses the standard exit monitoring hooks in `/tmp/agent-ml-exit/`, so completion callbacks won't fire automatically when these experiments finish. Consolidating the issues: the scierc_constrained registry is stale and needs correction, the nohup backend won't trigger completion hooks, and progress visibility is hampered by the logging format. On the positive side, xgrammar is patched and both DocRED experiments are running across 4 GPUs on two servers.
```

_source line 338 · 2026-05-16T05:27:46.690Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Both logs only show pad_token_id warnings and no progress lines `[N/998]`. The logs suggest the models are loaded and generating but progress output isn't being captured in the same format. Wait — the scripts write progress lines to stderr or stdout? The nohup redirect might only capture stdout.

Actually, looking at the previous logs from westd-16980, the progress lines like `[1/998] Skai TV: OK E=10 R=11 9.4s` were mixed with the pad_token_id warnings. The fact that the unconstrained log has many more pad_token_id lines (29 vs 11 for constrained) suggests the unconstrained is further along and making progress. 

The grep pattern was looking for lines starting with `[`, which catches both the transformer warnings and the progress markers. The unconstrained's 29 pad_token_id warnings suggest roughly 29 samples have been processed, while the constrained's 11 lines indicate it's still early in the run. Since the constrained job was just submitted, this makes sense — and the fact that it's generating multiple samples at all means the xgrammar patch likely held and prevented the crash that would've stopped it on the first sample.

But there's a stale registry issue I need to address: the scierc_constrained experiment shows RUNNING on westd-21461 but actually crashed with EXIT_CODE=1, while docred_unconstrained has now claimed cuda:0. The registry entry is outdated and needs to be marked FAILED, plus the job needs to be resubmitted — either on westd-21461 once the current jobs finish, or on westd-16980 after the Mistral SciERC unconstrained completes. The complication is that the xgrammar patch was only applied to westd-21461, so I need to track which machine gets the retry.

Actually, westd-16980 ran all the previous constrained experiments without hitting this xgrammar error, so scierc_constrained should be safe to rerun there after the unconstrained job finishes. Alternatively, now that xgrammar is patched on westd-21461, it could go there after DocRED wraps up. The other issue is that the worker used nohup instead of the standard tmux/submit_training_job wrapper, which means there's no exit file monitoring or automatic cleanup happening.
```

```text
Important findings:

1. **w6_mistral_scierc_unconstrained** - Now DONE with metrics:
   - E_F1=0.2728, R_F1=0.0366
   - DR_pct=56.94% (!), ET_pct=1.5%, RV_pct=0%
   - parse_fail_rate=0%, 551 samples
   - 56.94% domain-range violations — extremely high. This is the unconstrained baseline showing how badly Mistral generates relations outside the schema.

2. **w6_mistral_scierc_constrained** - Now PREPARING (re-set from failed). Being prepared for resubmission.
   
3. The GPU processes for both docred experiments have been cleaned up — the orphan PID 5576 on GPU 0 and PID 6794 on GPU 1 were killed by the diagnostic worker. The constrained docred run still shows RUNNING in the registry but its GPU process is gone, meaning it was terminated during cleanup along with the unconstrained orphan.

4. The docred_unconstrained experiment lost 39 lines of progress when its orphan process was killed, and now both DocRED runs on westd-21461 are dead with zero GPU utilization.

5. What's striking is the Mistral SciERC unconstrained results — the domain-range violation rate hit 56.94%, which is the highest across all models and datasets. That's a significant opportunity for OntoCoDec to demonstrate value if constrained decoding can reduce even a portion of those violations.

6. Comparing across models, Qwen's SciERC unconstrained DR sits around 2-4%, Llama's is higher but nowhere near 57%, and Llama's DocRED unconstrained is around 26% — so Mistral's 57% is genuinely exceptional and strengthens the generalization narrative if we can bring it down with constraints.

7. But there's a catch: Mistral's entity and relation F1 scores are abysmal at 27.28% and 3.66% respectively, compared to Qwen and Llama both hitting ~67% on SciERC. This suggests Mistral-7B-v0.3 is just fundamentally weaker at information extraction tasks, so the real question is whether constrained decoding can salvage the performance or if the model's baseline limitations are too severe.

Now I need to review the SciERC unconstrained metrics, confirm the westd-21461 cleanup, and identify what experiments are still missing.
```
